Continuing work from thread T-019b98be-6e9a-745b-bb1d-3285f1ec1460. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/view/mod.rs @src/markdown/preview.rs @src/model/editor_area.rs @docs/feature/markdown-preview.md

I implemented a Markdown Preview feature (F-170) for Token Editor with both native rendering and webview support.

The preview pane rendering happens in src/view/mod.rs in the render_preview_pane() function (around line 537-672). Currently it draws:
- A header bar with "Preview" text using tab_bar colors
- Content area with native markdown rendering as fallback (webview overlays on top)

The rendering approach mirrors render_editor_group() which renders tab bars, gutters, and text areas for editor groups. There's an opportunity to extract common "pane" patterns:
- Header bars with title text
- Border/separator lines
- Consistent padding and sizing
- Background fills

Key patterns in the view code:
- Uses TextPainter for text rendering with line_height and char_width
- Uses Frame for pixel-level fill_rect_px() calls
- Theme colors accessed via model.theme (tab_bar, editor, gutter, syntax colors)
- Rect structs define positioning (x, y, width, height)

The editor group rendering (render_editor_group around line 678) shows the pattern for tab bars with active/inactive states, which could inform a reusable pane header widget.

Tab bar rendering in render_tab_bar() (search for it in view/mod.rs) shows separator/border line patterns that should be consistent with preview pane headers.

lets look for oppertunities to improve the preview pane so it has a border under the pane header  and consistant sizing, look for oppertunities throughout the ui code to abstract a "pane" into a reusable widget

## Assistant

I've completed the refactoring. Here's a summary of the changes:

### Changes Made

#### 1. New `PaneHeaderLayout` struct in [geometry.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L553-L617)
- Provides consistent sizing for pane headers (title bar area)
- Uses same height as tab bars (`metrics.tab_bar_height`)
- Calculates title text position with proper padding
- Calculates border position at bottom of header

#### 2. New `PreviewLayout` struct in [geometry.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L623-L697)
- Similar to `GroupLayout` for editor groups
- Contains header layout, content rect, and padding
- Helper methods: `content_y()`, `content_w()`, `text_x()`, `max_text_width()`, `visible_lines()`

#### 3. New `render_pane_header()` helper in [mod.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L537-L566)
- Reusable widget for rendering pane headers
- Draws: background, title text, **bottom border** (matching tab bar style)
- Uses theme colors from `tab_bar`

#### 4. Refactored `render_preview_pane()` in [mod.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L569-L689)
- Now uses `PreviewLayout` for consistent geometry
- Uses `render_pane_header()` for the header (adds the missing border)
- Uses scaled metrics instead of hardcoded values

The preview pane now has a visible border under its header, consistent with editor tab bars, and the abstractions can be reused for future pane types.

- **Mouse Click Handling (Preview Pane)**: The `PreviewPane` is rendered in `src/view/mod.rs` (L530, L606) using a `geometry::Pane` layout (L629). Hit-testing for generic rectangles is defined in `src/model/editor_area.rs` (L55).
- **Middle-Click Handling**: Implemented in `src/runtime/app.rs` (L991-L1051). It specifically handles **closing tabs** by middle-clicking the tab bar (L1002-L1039) and starting **rectangle selection** in the editor area (L1042-L1047).

This feature was added in preparation of other ports like cef and servo. `protocol` (default): Enables [ `WebViewBuilder::with_custom_protocol` ] to define custom URL scheme for handling tasks like\nloading assets. `drag-drop` (default): Enables [ `WebViewBuilder::with_drag_drop_handler` ] to control the behavior when there are files\ninteracting with the window. `devtools` : Enables devtools on release builds. Devtools are always enabled in debug builds. On **macOS** , enabling devtools, requires calling private APIs so you should not enable this flag in release\nbuild if your app needs to publish to App Store. `transparent` : Transparent background on **macOS** requires calling private functions. ## Using `with_navigation_handler` in wry

The [`with_navigation_handler`](https://github.com/tauri-apps/wry/blob/main/src/lib.rs#L1240-L1247) method in [`WebViewBuilder`](https://github.com/tauri-apps/wry/blob/main/src/lib.rs#L858-L876) allows you to intercept navigation events and decide whether to allow or block them. Here's how to use it to open external URLs in the system default browser instead of navigating within the webview:

### Method Signature

```rust
pub fn with_navigation_handler(mut self, callback: impl Fn(String) -> bool + 'static) -> Self
```

The closure:
- **Parameter**: `String` - the URL being navigated to
- **Return value**: `bool`
  - `true` = allow navigation in the webview
  - `false` = block navigation in the webview

### Complete Example

```rust
use tao::{
    event::{Event, WindowEvent},
    event_loop::{ControlFlow, EventLoop},
    window::WindowBuilder,
};
use wry::WebViewBuilder;

fn main() -> wry::Result<()> {
    let event_loop = EventLoop::new();
    let window = WindowBuilder::new().build(&event_loop).unwrap();

    let _webview = WebViewBuilder::new()
        .with_url("http://example.com")
        .with_navigation_handler(|url| {
            // Check if the URL is an external link
            if is_external_url(&url) {
                // Open in system default browser
                let _ = open_in_browser(&url);
                // Return false to prevent navigation in webview
                false
            } else {
                // Allow internal navigation within the webview
                true
            }
        })
        .build(&window)?;

    event_loop.run(move |event, _, control_flow| {
        *control_flow = ControlFlow::Wait;

        if let Event::WindowEvent {
            event: WindowEvent::CloseRequested,
            ..
        } = event
        {
            *control_flow = ControlFlow::Exit;
        }
    });
}

fn is_external_url(url: &str) -> bool {
    // Example: treat URLs not starting with your app's domain as external
    !url.starts_with("http://example.com") &&
    !url.starts_with("https://example.com") &&
    !url.starts_with("file://")
}

fn open_in_browser(url: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    // Cross-platform solution using webbrowser crate (add to Cargo.toml)
    webbrowser::open(url)?;
    Ok(())
}
```

### With `webbrowser` Crate

Add this to your `Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[dependencies]
webbrowser = "1.0"
```

Then use it in your handler:

```rust
.with_navigation_handler(|url| {
    if url.starts_with("http://") || url.starts_with("https://") {
        if !url.contains("example.com") {
            // Open external URLs in default browser
            let _ = webbrowser::open(&url);
            return false; // Block navigation in webview
        }
    }
    true // Allow internal navigation
})
```

### More Sophisticated URL Filtering

```rust
use url::Url;

.with_navigation_handler(|url_str| {
    // Parse and check the URL
    if let Ok(url) = Url::parse(&url_str) {
        let host = url.host_str().unwrap_or("");

        // List of allowed internal hosts
        let allowed_hosts = ["localhost", "127.0.0.1", "example.com"];

        if !allowed_hosts.iter().any(|&h| host.contains(h)) {
            // External URL - open in default browser
            let _ = webbrowser::open(&url_str);
            return false;
        }
    }

    true // Allow navigation in webview
})
```

### Key Points

1. **Return `true`** to allow navigation within the webview
2. **Return `false`** to block navigation (when opening externally)
3. The handler is called before navigation occurs, giving you control
4. Use `webbrowser` crate for cross-platform browser opening
5. This works on all platforms (Windows, macOS, Linux, Android, iOS)

For more details, see the [WebViewBuilder documentation](https://docs.rs/wry/latest/wry/webview/struct.WebViewBuilder.html#method.with_navigation_handler).
```

### Summary

#### 1. New `Pane` abstraction in [geometry.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/geometry.rs#L548-L860)

A reusable layout primitive with:
- **`PaneBorders`** - configurable borders (top/bottom/left/right)
- **`PaneInsets`** - content padding
- **`Pane`** struct with:
  - Optional header (uses `tab_bar_height`)
  - Header border
  - Content area with insets
  - Helper methods: `content_x()`, `content_y()`, `content_width()`, `content_height()`, `visible_lines()`, `is_in_header()`, etc.

#### 2. New `render_pane()` helper in [mod.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L537-L602)

Renders pane chrome (background, header, borders) - reusable for any pane type.

#### 3. Middle-click to close preview in [app.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L1001-L1014)

Clicking middle button on preview header now closes the preview pane.

#### 4. Links open in default browser in [webview.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/webview.rs#L42-L53)

Added `with_navigation_handler` to intercept `http://` and `https://` links and open them with `open::that()` instead of navigating within the webview.

## User

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01VTwpEtEu4AabgTtD37dwwR`

```
1) TL;DR
Tie preview to an `EditorGroup` (and its active `DocumentId`), not just the document; remove it as a “free‑floating” layout node; and treat the webview as just another rendering of that group’s preview area that is hidden whenever a modal/command palette is active.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Fix the data model: preview is attached to a group

**1. Extend `EditorGroup` and `PreviewPane`**

In `EditorGroup`:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct EditorGroup {
    pub id: GroupId,
    pub tabs: Vec<Tab>,
    pub active_tab_index: usize,
    pub rect: Rect,

    /// Preview attached to this group (if any)
    pub attached_preview: Option<PreviewId>,
}
```

In `PreviewPane` (markdown/preview.rs):

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PreviewPane {
    pub id: PreviewId,
    pub document_id: DocumentId,
    pub rendered_html: String,
    pub rendered_lines: Vec<RenderedLine>,
    pub scroll_offset: usize,
    pub scroll_sync_enabled: bool,
    pub rect: Rect,
    pub last_revision: u64,

    /// The group this preview is visually attached to
    pub group_id: GroupId,
}
```

And update `PreviewPane::new`:

```rust
impl PreviewPane {
    pub fn new(id: PreviewId, document_id: DocumentId, group_id: GroupId) -> Self {
        Self {
            id,
            document_id,
            group_id,
            rendered_html: String::new(),
            rendered_lines: Vec::new(),
            scroll_offset: 0,
            scroll_sync_enabled: true,
            rect: Rect::default(),
            last_revision: 0,
        }
    }
}
```

**Why:** This makes preview identity `(group_id, document_id)`, instead of a global “preview for document X”, which is what causes orphaning and wrong lifetime today.

---

### B. Fix the layout: preview is a split of a specific group

You already have `LayoutNode::{Group, Split, Preview}` and helper `replace_group_with_split`. The core problems are:

- The layout tree does not know *which group* the preview belongs to beyond that helper call.
- There’s no easy way to find “the preview for this group” later when the active tab or document changes.

Use `attached_preview` as the source of truth.

**2. Opening a preview for a group**

Add methods on `EditorArea` (editor_area.rs):

```rust
impl EditorArea {
    pub fn open_preview_for_focused_group(&mut self) {
        let group_id = self.focused_group_id;
        self.open_preview_for_group(group_id);
    }

    pub fn open_preview_for_group(&mut self, group_id: GroupId) {
        let Some(group) = self.groups.get(&group_id) else { return; };
        let Some(editor_id) = group.active_editor_id() else { return; };
        let Some(editor) = self.editors.get(&editor_id) else { return; };
        let Some(doc_id) = editor.document_id else { return; };

        // Only allow preview for markdown docs (you probably already have a check)
        if !self.is_markdown_document(doc_id) {
            return;
        }

        // If a preview is already attached to this group, reuse or retarget it
        if let Some(existing_pid) = group.attached_preview {
            let preview = self.previews.get_mut(&existing_pid).unwrap();
            preview.document_id = doc_id;
            preview.last_revision = 0;
            preview.scroll_offset = 0;
            return;
        }

        // Create new PreviewPane
        let preview_id = PreviewId(self.next_preview_id);
        self.next_preview_id += 1;

        let preview = PreviewPane::new(preview_id, doc_id, group_id);
        self.previews.insert(preview_id, preview);

        // Attach to group
        self.groups
            .get_mut(&group_id)
            .unwrap()
            .attached_preview = Some(preview_id);

        // Replace the Group node with Split(Group, Preview) if not already split
        self.layout = replace_group_with_split(
            std::mem::take(&mut self.layout),
            group_id,
            preview_id,
        );
    }

    pub fn toggle_focused_preview(&mut self) -> bool {
        let group_id = self.focused_group_id;
        if let Some(preview_id) = self.groups
            .get(&group_id)
            .and_then(|g| g.attached_preview)
        {
            self.close_preview(preview_id);
            false
        } else {
            self.open_preview_for_group(group_id);
            true
        }
    }
}
```

**3. Closing a preview (detach from group + layout + webview)**

Update `close_preview` to also clean up `attached_preview` and the layout:

```rust
impl EditorArea {
    pub fn close_preview(&mut self, preview_id: PreviewId) {
        // Get its group before removing
        let group_id = match self.previews.get(&preview_id) {
            Some(p) => p.group_id,
            None => return,
        };

        // Drop preview state
        self.previews.remove(&preview_id);

        // Detach from group
        if let Some(group) = self.groups.get_mut(&group_id) {
            if group.attached_preview == Some(preview_id) {
                group.attached_preview = None;
            }
        }

        // Remove Preview node from layout and “unsplit” if appropriate
        self.layout = remove_preview_from_layout(
            std::mem::take(&mut self.layout),
            preview_id,
            group_id,
        );

        // Also close webview (see section E)
        // model.webview_manager.close_webview(preview_id);
    }
}
```

You’ll need a counterpart to `replace_group_with_split`, e.g.:

```rust
/// Recursively remove a preview node; if a split ends up with a single Group child,
/// collapse it back to just that Group.
fn remove_preview_from_layout(
    node: LayoutNode,
    preview_id: PreviewId,
    group_id: GroupId,
) -> LayoutNode {
    match node {
        LayoutNode::Split(mut container) => {
            // Recursively process children
            container.children = container
                .children
                .into_iter()
                .map(|child| remove_preview_from_layout(child, preview_id, group_id))
                .collect();

            // Filter out the Preview node with this id
            container.children.retain(|child| !matches!(child, LayoutNode::Preview(pid) if *pid == preview_id));

            // If there's exactly one child left and it’s the target group, collapse
            if container.children.len() == 1 {
                container.children.into_iter().next().unwrap()
            } else {
                LayoutNode::Split(container)
            }
        }
        other => other,
    }
}
```

(Adjust to match your existing layout helpers.)

**4. Hit testing for previews**

You can keep `LayoutNode::Preview` and the existing `preview_at_point` logic. Just ensure `PreviewPane.rect` is updated during layout (see next step), and the group association is in `PreviewPane.group_id`.

---

### C. Keep preview lifetime in sync with tabs and documents

**5. Close preview when switching active tab to a non‑markdown file**

Wherever you change `active_tab_index` for a group (e.g. in `EditorArea::activate_tab`), call a helper after the switch:

```rust
impl EditorArea {
    pub fn on_group_active_tab_changed(&mut self, group_id: GroupId) {
        let Some(group) = self.groups.get(&group_id) else { return; };
        let Some(editor_id) = group.active_editor_id() else { return; };
        let Some(editor) = self.editors.get(&editor_id) else { return; };
        let Some(doc_id) = editor.document_id else { return; };

        // If there is an attached preview for this group
        if let Some(preview_id) = group.attached_preview {
            let preview = self.previews.get(&preview_id).unwrap();

            // If we switched away from the preview's document or to a non-markdown file,
            // close the preview.
            if preview.document_id != doc_id || !self.is_markdown_document(doc_id) {
                // avoid borrow conflicts by collecting id first
                drop(preview);
                drop(group);
                self.close_preview(preview_id);
            }
        }
    }
}
```

Ensure all tab‑change paths (mouse click on tab, keyboard cycling, “open file in tab”) call `on_group_active_tab_changed`.

**6. Close preview when a document is closed**

Where you close a document or tab (e.g. `EditorArea::close_tab`, `EditorArea::close_document`):

```rust
impl EditorArea {
    fn close_previews_for_document(&mut self, doc_id: DocumentId) {
        // Collect first to avoid mutating while iterating
        let preview_ids: Vec<PreviewId> = self.previews
            .iter()
            .filter_map(|(&pid, p)| if p.document_id == doc_id { Some(pid) } else { None })
            .collect();

        for pid in preview_ids {
            self.close_preview(pid);
        }
    }
}
```

Call this before/while you remove the document from `self.documents`.

This directly addresses bug (2): no more orphan preview pane, because closing the markdown document always tears down any previews for it.

---

### D. Wire the `PreviewMsg` update logic to groups instead of just documents

Current `preview::update_preview` uses `focused_document_id()` and `find_preview_for_document`. Both are document‑centric and ignore which group is showing the preview.

**7. Make preview operations group‑centric**

In `AppModel::editor_area`, add helpers:

```rust
impl EditorArea {
    pub fn focused_group_id(&self) -> GroupId {
        self.focused_group_id
    }

    pub fn find_preview_for_group(&self, group_id: GroupId) -> Option<PreviewId> {
        self.groups
            .get(&group_id)
            .and_then(|g| g.attached_preview)
    }

    pub fn preview_for_group_mut(&mut self, group_id: GroupId) -> Option<&mut PreviewPane> {
        let pid = self.groups.get(&group_id)?.attached_preview?;
        self.previews.get_mut(&pid)
    }
}
```

Then, update `preview::update_preview`:

```rust
pub fn update_preview(model: &mut AppModel, msg: PreviewMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    let group_id = model.editor_area.focused_group_id();

    match msg {
        PreviewMsg::Toggle => {
            let opened = model.editor_area.toggle_focused_preview();
            tracing::info!("Markdown preview toggled: {}", if opened { "opened" } else { "closed" });
            Some(Cmd::Redraw)
        }
        PreviewMsg::Open => {
            model.editor_area.open_preview_for_focused_group();
            Some(Cmd::Redraw)
        }
        PreviewMsg::Close => {
            if let Some(preview_id) = model.editor_area.find_preview_for_group(group_id) {
                model.editor_area.close_preview(preview_id);
                Some(Cmd::Redraw)
            } else {
                None
            }
        }
        PreviewMsg::Refresh => Some(Cmd::Redraw),

        PreviewMsg::ScrollToLine(line) => {
            if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview_for_group_mut(group_id) {
                if preview.scroll_sync_enabled {
                    preview.scroll_offset = line;
                    return Some(Cmd::Redraw);
                }
            }
            None
        }

        PreviewMsg::SyncFromPreview(line) => {
            if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview_for_group_mut(group_id) {
                if preview.scroll_sync_enabled {
                    model.editor_mut().viewport.top_line = line;
                    return Some(Cmd::Redraw);
                }
            }
            None
        }

        PreviewMsg::ToggleSync => {
            if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview_for_group_mut(group_id) {
                preview.scroll_sync_enabled = !preview.scroll_sync_enabled;
                tracing::info!(
                    "Preview scroll sync: {}",
                    if preview.scroll_sync_enabled { "enabled" } else { "disabled" }
                );
                return Some(Cmd::Redraw);
            }
            None
        }
    }
}
```

This ensures all preview actions are scoped to “the preview attached to the currently focused group”.

---

### E. Respect modal z‑order: integrate webview visibility with UI state

The z‑order bug comes from `WebView::build_as_child(window)`: child windows are composited by the OS after your softbuffer frame, so they always appear over the in‑process command palette.

Simplest structural fix with your existing architecture:

**8. Add visibility controls to `WebviewManager`**

In `view/webview.rs`:

```rust
impl WebviewManager {
    pub fn set_visible(&self, preview_id: PreviewId, visible: bool) {
        if let Some(webview) = self.webviews.get(&preview_id) {
            let _ = webview.set_visible(visible);
        }
    }

    pub fn set_visible_for_all(&self, visible: bool) {
        for (&pid, _) in &self.webviews {
            self.set_visible(pid, visible);
        }
    }
}
```

(If `wry::WebView` lacks `set_visible`, you can hack it by shrinking bounds to zero when hidden and restoring when shown.)

**9. Hide webviews when a modal / command palette is active**

Where you already compute effective damage and know that a modal is shown (in `Renderer::compute_effective_damage` / or right before/after you render the frame), add:

```rust
fn render_frame(&mut self, model: &AppModel, perf: &PerfStats) {
    let damage = self.compute_effective_damage(&model.damage, model, perf);

    // Hide previews when a modal/command palette is active
    let show_previews = model.ui.active_modal.is_none();
    model.webview_manager.set_visible_for_all(show_previews);

    // ... existing drawing of editor, overlays, etc.
}
```

Now, whenever the command palette (or any modal) is up, all preview webviews are hidden, so they cannot appear above it. When the modal is closed, webviews are restored.

**10. Keep webview bounds in sync with preview.rect**

During layout, after you compute `preview.rect` (either in `compute_layout_node` when you encounter `LayoutNode::Preview`, or right after), update the webview:

```rust
if let LayoutNode::Preview(pid) = node {
    if let Some(preview) = self.previews.get_mut(pid) {
        preview.rect = rect;
        // later, in a place where you have access to WebviewManager and scale factor:
        webview_manager.update_bounds(*pid, rect, renderer.scale_factor());
    }
}
```

This keeps the webview tightly attached to the group’s split area.

---

3) Rationale and trade-offs

- The core structural issue is that a preview is keyed only by `DocumentId` and represented as an independent `LayoutNode::Preview`, while UX semantics are per‑group. This mismatch causes orphan previews and previews persisting when the active tab changes.
- Attaching preview to `EditorGroup` via `attached_preview: Option<PreviewId>` and storing `group_id` in `PreviewPane` aligns the data model with the UX: “this group is currently split with a preview of its active markdown document”.
- Keeping the layout tree as `Group`/`Split`/`Preview` avoids a full rewrite; you just add invariants (a `Preview` node always belongs to exactly one `group_id`) and maintain them in `open_preview_for_group`/`close_preview`.
- Hiding webviews when modals are up is simpler than redesigning modal rendering or avoiding child windows, and it fully fixes “preview on top of command palette”.

Effort: **M–L (1–2 days)** including wiring into all tab/document close paths and verifying edge cases.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- **Risk:** Missed code paths where active tab changes → preview stays open for the wrong document.
  **Guardrail:** Centralize tab activation in one `EditorArea::set_active_tab(group_id, tab_index)` that always calls `on_group_active_tab_changed`.
- **Risk:** Layout and `attached_preview` get out of sync.
  **Guardrail:** In debug builds, extend `assert_invariants` to check:
  - Every `PreviewPane.group_id`’s group has `attached_preview == Some(preview.id)`.
  - Every `attached_preview` has a corresponding `PreviewPane` and a `LayoutNode::Preview` under the same parent split as the group.
- **Risk:** Webview visibility toggling flickers when opening/closing modals rapidly.
  **Guardrail:** Only call `set_visible_for_all` when the modal visibility actually changes (track previous modal state).

---

5) When to consider the advanced path

Consider a more complex design if:

- You want **per‑group adjustable preview split ratios** and drag handles independent of the global split system.
- You need **multiple previews for the same document** in different groups with independent scroll state.
- You want the preview to be a true “view mode” (like your CSV mode) rather than an attached pane.

---

6) Optional advanced path (outline)

An alternative, more unified design:

- Remove `LayoutNode::Preview` entirely.
- Model preview as a second `EditorState` (same `document_id`, `view_mode = MarkdownPreview`) in its own `EditorGroup`, and open that group via your existing split mechanics. Now preview is literally a specialized editor pane.
- Reuse all existing group layout, splitter dragging, focus dimming, etc., and render preview via `render_preview_pane` whenever an editor’s `view_mode` is `Preview`.

This yields a cleaner architecture but is a larger refactor. The simple path above gives you correct lifecycles and z‑ordering with minimal disruption.
```

Markdown preview update handler\n\nuse crate::commands::Cmd;\nuse crate::messages::PreviewMsg;\nuse crate::model::AppModel;\n\npub fn update_preview(model: &mut AppModel, msg: PreviewMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n    let group_id = model.editor_area.focused_group_id;\n\n    match msg {\n        PreviewMsg::Toggle => {\n            let opened = model.editor_area.toggle_focused_preview();\n            tracing::info!(\n                \"Markdown preview toggled: {}\",\n                if opened { \"opened\" } else { \"closed\" }\n            );\n            Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n        }\n        PreviewMsg::Open => {\n            model.editor_area.open_preview_for_focused_group();\n            tracing::info!(\"Markdown preview opened\");\n            Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n        }\n        PreviewMsg::Close => {\n            if let Some(preview_id) = model.editor_area.find_preview_for_group(group_id) {\n                model.editor_area.close_preview(preview_id);\n                tracing::info!(\"Markdown preview closed\");\n                Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n            } else {\n                None\n            }\n        }\n        PreviewMsg::Refresh => Some(Cmd::Redraw),\n        PreviewMsg::ScrollToLine(line) => {\n            if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview_for_group_mut(group_id) {\n                if preview.scroll_sync_enabled {\n                    preview.scroll_offset = line;\n                    return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n                }\n            }\n            None\n        }\n        PreviewMsg::SyncFromPreview(line) => {\n            if let Some(preview_id) = model.editor_area.find_preview_for_group(group_id) {\n                if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview(preview_id) {\n                    if preview.scroll_sync_enabled {\n                        model.editor_mut().viewport.top_line = line;\n                        return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            None\n        }\n        PreviewMsg::ToggleSync => {\n            if let Some(preview) = model.editor_area.preview_for_group_mut(group_id) {\n                preview.scroll_sync_enabled = !preview.scroll_sync_enabled;\n                tracing::info!(\n                    \"Preview scroll sync: {}\",\n                    if preview.scroll_sync_enabled {\n                        \"enabled\"\n                    } else {\n                        \"disabled\"\n                    }\n                );\n                return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n            }\n            None\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}

## Assistant

### Summary of Changes

I've restructured the markdown preview to be **group-centric** rather than document-centric, fixing the lifecycle issues:

#### Data Model Changes

1. **`EditorGroup`** now has `attached_preview: Option<PreviewId>` - ties preview to a specific group
2. **`PreviewPane`** now has `group_id: GroupId` - tracks which group owns it

#### New EditorArea Methods ([editor_area.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/editor_area.rs#L400-L600))

- `find_preview_for_group()` / `focused_group_preview()` - find preview by group
- `open_preview_for_group()` / `open_preview_for_focused_group()` - create/retarget preview
- `on_group_active_tab_changed()` - **closes preview when switching to different document**
- `close_previews_for_document()` - **closes previews when document is closed**

#### Lifecycle Wiring

- **Tab switching** now calls `on_group_active_tab_changed()` to close preview when navigating away from the source document
- **Tab close** checks if the closed tab was showing the preview's document and closes it
- **Preview operations** are now scoped to the focused group, not global

#### Modal Z-Order Fix ([app.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L1185-L1188))

- Added `webview_manager.set_all_visible(show_webviews)` to hide webviews when command palette or other modals are open
- Webviews are shown again when modals close

#### Also Completed Earlier

- **Pane abstraction** for consistent UI widget rendering
- **Middle-click to close preview** on the preview header
- **Links open in default browser** via `with_navigation_handler`

## Assistant

Fixed. Now clicking anywhere in the preview pane will:
1. Consume the left-click (not pass through to editor)
2. Keep the editor focused so Cmd+W and other keyboard shortcuts still work

The change is in [app.rs](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L828-L836) - checks if click is inside any preview rect and calls `focus_editor()` before consuming the event.
